) to a mapping

\mathcal{Q}^{\perp}\to\mathfrak{st}(\mathcal{T})^{\perp}\to\mathrm{SO}(\mathcal{T});\quad(s_{1},s_{2})\mapsto\mathchoice{\displaystyle\boldsymbol{p}}{\textstyle\boldsymbol{p}}{\scriptstyle\boldsymbol{p}}{\scriptscriptstyle\boldsymbol{p}}:=s_{1}\mathchoice{\displaystyle\boldsymbol{k}}{\textstyle\boldsymbol{k}}{\scriptstyle\boldsymbol{k}}{\scriptscriptstyle\boldsymbol{k}}_{1}+s_{2}\mathchoice{\displaystyle\boldsymbol{k}}{\textstyle\boldsymbol{k}}{\scriptstyle\boldsymbol{k}}{\scriptscriptstyle\boldsymbol{k}}_{2}\mapsto\mathchoice{\displaystyle\mathsfbfit{P}}{\textstyle\mathsfbfit{P}}{\scriptstyle\mathsfbfit{P}}{\scriptscriptstyle\mathsfbfit{P}}\mapsto\mathchoice{\displaystyle\mathsfbfit{V}}{\textstyle\mathsfbfit{V}}{\scriptstyle\mathsfbfit{V}}{\scriptscriptstyle\mathsfbfit{V}}=\exp(\mathchoice{\displaystyle\mathsfbfit{P}}{\textstyle\mathsfbfit{P}}{\scriptstyle\mathsfbfit{P}}{\scriptscriptstyle\mathsfbfit{P}}).

(122)

Appendix C
Appendix: Notation

With physical space given by
\mathcal{V}\equiv\mathbb{R}^{d}
(the physically interesting instances
are
d=1,2,3
), we are interested in elements of various tensor powers
\mathcal{V}^{\otimes q}
.

Denote vectors as
\mathchoice{\displaystyle\boldsymbol{x}}{\textstyle\boldsymbol{x}}{\scriptstyle\boldsymbol{x}}{\scriptscriptstyle\boldsymbol{x}}=(x_{i})\in\mathcal{V}
with lower case boldface,
second order tensors (identified with linear maps)
as
\mathchoice{\displaystyle\boldsymbol{Q}}{\textstyle\boldsymbol{Q}}{\scriptstyle\boldsymbol{Q}}{\scriptscriptstyle\boldsymbol{Q}}=(Q_{ik})\in\mathcal{V}\otimes\mathcal{V}=\mathcal{V}^{\otimes 2}\cong\mathscr{L}(\mathcal{V})

with upper case boldface,
the identification given via the matrix-vector product
\mathchoice{\displaystyle\boldsymbol{y}}{\textstyle\boldsymbol{y}}{\scriptstyle\boldsymbol{y}}{\scriptscriptstyle\boldsymbol{y}}=(y_{i})=\mathchoice{\displaystyle\boldsymbol{Q}}{\textstyle\boldsymbol{Q}}{\scriptstyle\boldsymbol{Q}}{\scriptscriptstyle\boldsymbol{Q}}\mathchoice{\displaystyle\boldsymbol{x}}{\textstyle\boldsymbol{x}}{\scriptstyle\boldsymbol{x}}{\scriptscriptstyle\boldsymbol{x}}=(Q_{ik}x_{k})
,
where Einstein’s summation convention over repeated indices is used.
Linear maps are identified with the associated matrices.
Matrix-matrix multiplication resp. the concatenation of maps in
\mathscr{L}(\mathcal{V})
is denoted
similarly as
\mathchoice{\displaystyle\boldsymbol{P}}{\textstyle\boldsymbol{P}}{\scriptstyle\boldsymbol{P}}{\scriptscriptstyle\boldsymbol{P}}=\mathchoice{\displaystyle\boldsymbol{Q}}{\textstyle\boldsymbol{Q}}{\scriptstyle\boldsymbol{Q}}{\scriptscriptstyle\boldsymbol{Q}}\mathchoice{\displaystyle\boldsymbol{R}}{\textstyle\boldsymbol{R}}{\scriptstyle\boldsymbol{R}}{\scriptscriptstyle\boldsymbol{R}}=(Q_{ik}R_{kj})=(P_{ij})
.

Tensors of order
q>2
will be denoted as
\mathchoice{\displaystyle\mathbf{w}}{\textstyle\mathbf{w}}{\scriptstyle\mathbf{w}}{\scriptscriptstyle\mathbf{w}}=(\mathrm{w}_{i_{1}i_{2}\dots i_{q}})=(\mathrm{w}_{\mathfrak{i}})\in\mathcal{V}^{\otimes q}
in lower case bold face roman, and their components
in lower case normal face roman, with a multi-index
\mathfrak{i}=(i_{1},i_{2},\dots,i_{q})\in\mathbb{N}^{q}

denoted in lower case fraktur.

C.1
Kelvin notation

Applying the foregoing to the case of linear elasticity, the state of the material
is described by the symmetric infinitesimal strain and Cauchy stress tensors

\mathchoice{\displaystyle\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}}{\textstyle\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}}{\scriptstyle\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}}{\scriptscriptstyle\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}},\mathchoice{\displaystyle\boldsymbol{\sigma}}{\textstyle\boldsymbol{\sigma}}{\scriptstyle\boldsymbol{\sigma}}{\scriptscriptstyle\boldsymbol{\sigma}}\in\mathcal{S}
, where

\mathcal{S}:=\mathscr{L}_{s}(\mathcal{V})=\mathfrak{sym}(\mathcal{V})\subset\mathcal{V}^{\otimes 2}

is the space
of symmetric 2nd order tensors / matrices resp. symmetric linear maps of
\mathcal{V}
:

\mathchoice{\displaystyle\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}}{\textstyle\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}}{\scriptstyle\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}}{\scriptscriptstyle\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}}=(\varepsilon_{ij})=(\varepsilon_{ji})=\mathchoice{\displaystyle\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}}{\textstyle\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}}{\scriptstyle\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}}{\scriptscriptstyle\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}}^{\mathchoice{\displaystyle\mathsf{T}}{\textstyle\mathsf{T}}{\scriptstyle\mathsf{T}}{\scriptscriptstyle\mathsf{T}}}
, and similarly for
\mathchoice{\displaystyle\boldsymbol{\sigma}}{\textstyle\boldsymbol{\sigma}}{\scriptstyle\boldsymbol{\sigma}}{\scriptscriptstyle\boldsymbol{\sigma}}
.
In
\mathcal{S}
the inner product is induced by the one in
\mathcal{V}
, and denoted by

\mathchoice{\displaystyle\boldsymbol{\sigma}}{\textstyle\boldsymbol{\sigma}}{\scriptstyle\boldsymbol{\sigma}}{\scriptscriptstyle\boldsymbol{\sigma}}:\mathchoice{\displaystyle\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}}{\textstyle\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}}{\scriptstyle\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}}{\scriptscriptstyle\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}}=\left\langle\mathchoice{\displaystyle\boldsymbol{\sigma}}{\textstyle\boldsymbol{\sigma}}{\scriptstyle\boldsymbol{\sigma}}{\scriptscriptstyle\boldsymbol{\sigma}},\mathchoice{\displaystyle\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}}{\textstyle\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}}{\scriptstyle\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}}{\scriptscriptstyle\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}}\right\rangle=(\sigma_{ik}\varepsilon_{ik})
,
a double contraction.

The strain and stress tensor are connected through Hooke’s generalised law
Eqs.(
6
) and (
123
) by the elasticity tensor

\mathchoice{\displaystyle\mathbf{c}}{\textstyle\mathbf{c}}{\scriptstyle\mathbf{c}}{\scriptscriptstyle\mathbf{c}}=(\mathrm{c}_{i_{1}i_{2}i_{3}i_{4}})\in\mathcal{E}:=\mathscr{L}_{s}(\mathcal{S})\subset\mathcal{V}^{\otimes 4}
,
which according to Eq.(
1
) can be seen as a symmetric linear map on
\mathcal{S}
.
Hooke’s elasticity tensor
\mathchoice{\displaystyle\mathbf{c}}{\textstyle\mathbf{c}}{\scriptstyle\mathbf{c}}{\scriptscriptstyle\mathbf{c}}
is a 4th order tensor in the space

\mathchoice{\displaystyle\mathbf{c}}{\textstyle\mathbf{c}}{\scriptstyle\mathbf{c}}{\scriptscriptstyle\mathbf{c}}\in\mathcal{E}\subset\mathcal{V}^{\otimes 4}
with major (physical) symmetry

\mathrm{c}_{ik\ell m}=\mathrm{c}_{\ell mik}
, as it is a symmetric linear map

\mathchoice{\displaystyle\mathbf{c}}{\textstyle\mathbf{c}}{\scriptstyle\mathbf{c}}{\scriptscriptstyle\mathbf{c}}\in\mathscr{L}_{s}(\mathcal{S})
on
\mathcal{S}
(cf. Eq.(
2
)), and minor symmetries

\mathrm{c}_{ik\ell m}=\mathrm{c}_{ki\ell m}=\mathrm{c}_{ikm\ell}
,
as it operates on symmetric second-order tensors in
\mathcal{S}
, cf.
[
118
]
.

\text{Hooke's generalised law: }\quad\mathchoice{\displaystyle\boldsymbol{\sigma}}{\textstyle\boldsymbol{\sigma}}{\scriptstyle\boldsymbol{\sigma}}{\scriptscriptstyle\boldsymbol{\sigma}}=(\sigma_{i_{1}i_{2}})=\mathchoice{\displaystyle\mathbf{c}}{\textstyle\mathbf{c}}{\scriptstyle\mathbf{c}}{\scriptscriptstyle\mathbf{c}}:\mathchoice{\displaystyle\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}}{\textstyle\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}}{\scriptstyle\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}}{\scriptscriptstyle\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}}=(\mathrm{c}_{i_{1}i_{2}i_{3}i_{4}}\varepsilon_{i_{3}i_{4}}).

(123)

For
\dim\,\mathcal{V}=d=3
one has
\dim\,\mathcal{S}=6
, and for
\dim\,\mathcal{V}=d=2

it is
\dim\,\mathcal{S}=3
.
For
\dim\,\mathcal{V}=d=3
one has
\dim\mathcal{E}=21
, and for
\dim\,\mathcal{V}=d=2

it is
\dim\mathcal{E}=6
. The elasticity tensor is usually also SPD, see Eq.(
3
).

Hooke’s law in Kelvin’s matrix notation:

As already alluded to following Eq.(
1
) as well as Eqs.(
6
) and (
123
),
one may associate a linear map to the elasticity tensor
\mathchoice{\displaystyle\mathbf{c}}{\textstyle\mathbf{c}}{\scriptstyle\mathbf{c}}{\scriptscriptstyle\mathbf{c}}\in\mathcal{E}
.
It is often simplest done by choosing a basis in the strain-stress space

\mathcal{S}
. One such way to assign a matrix to the elasticity
tensor
\mathchoice{\displaystyle\mathbf{c}}{\textstyle\mathbf{c}}{\scriptstyle\mathbf{c}}{\scriptscriptstyle\mathbf{c}}\in\mathcal{E}
is the well-known

Voigt notation
by choosing different bases in
\mathcal{S}

for strains and for stresses. Unfortunately, the resulting matrix does not transform as a
tensor
[
113
,
118
]
, and also would make the formulation of eigenvectors
awkward
[
76
]
.
Therefore it is advantageous to choose the same basis for stresses and strains, and
thereby defining a new space
\mathcal{T}=\mathbb{R}^{k}
, where
k=\dim\,\mathcal{S}
,
i.e.
k=6
for
d=3
and
k=3
in case
d=2
. This gives a one-to-one
correspondence from
\mathcal{S}
to
\mathcal{T}
,
a map which will be designated by
\mathchoice{\displaystyle\mathsf{vrep}}{\textstyle\mathsf{vrep}}{\scriptstyle\mathsf{vrep}}{\scriptscriptstyle\mathsf{vrep}}:\mathcal{S}\to\mathcal{T}
.
This is classically known as the
Kelvin notation
, cf.
[
113
,
118
,
76
]
.

Kelvin notation in 3D:

Following Kelvin
[
177
,
178
]
, see also
[
75
,
76
]
for
an account in modern notation, one chooses in 3D for
d=3
and
k=6=\dim\mathcal{S}

one
basis for strains and stresses in
\mathcal{S}

[
113
,
118
]
,
such that the stress and strain
\mathchoice{\displaystyle\boldsymbol{\sigma}}{\textstyle\boldsymbol{\sigma}}{\scriptstyle\boldsymbol{\sigma}}{\scriptscriptstyle\boldsymbol{\sigma}},\mathchoice{\displaystyle\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}}{\textstyle\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}}{\scriptstyle\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}}{\scriptscriptstyle\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}}\in\mathcal{S}
is represented by

\displaystyle\mathchoice{\displaystyle\mathsfbfit{s}}{\textstyle\mathsfbfit{s}}{\scriptstyle\mathsfbfit{s}}{\scriptscriptstyle\mathsfbfit{s}}

\displaystyle:=(\mathchoice{\displaystyle\mathsfit{s}}{\textstyle\mathsfit{s}}{\scriptstyle\mathsfit{s}}{\scriptscriptstyle\mathsfit{s}}_{i})=\mathchoice{\displaystyle\mathsf{vrep}}{\textstyle\mathsf{vrep}}{\scriptstyle\mathsf{vrep}}{\scriptscriptstyle\mathsf{vrep}}(\mathchoice{\displaystyle\boldsymbol{\sigma}}{\textstyle\boldsymbol{\sigma}}{\scriptstyle\boldsymbol{\sigma}}{\scriptscriptstyle\boldsymbol{\sigma}})=\mathchoice{\displaystyle\mathsf{vrep}}{\textstyle\mathsf{vrep}}{\scriptstyle\mathsf{vrep}}{\scriptscriptstyle\mathsf{vrep}}((\sigma_{ij})):=[\sigma_{11},\sigma_{22},\sigma_{33},\sqrt{2}\,\sigma_{23},\sqrt{2}\,\sigma_{13},\sqrt{2}\,\sigma_{12}]^{\mathchoice{\displaystyle\mathsf{T}}{\textstyle\mathsf{T}}{\scriptstyle\mathsf{T}}{\scriptscriptstyle\mathsf{T}}}\in\mathcal{T}

(124)

\displaystyle\mathchoice{\displaystyle\mathsfbfit{e}}{\textstyle\mathsfbfit{e}}{\scriptstyle\mathsfbfit{e}}{\scriptscriptstyle\mathsfbfit{e}}

\displaystyle:=(\mathchoice{\displaystyle\mathsfit{e}}{\textstyle\mathsfit{e}}{\scriptstyle\mathsfit{e}}{\scriptscriptstyle\mathsfit{e}}_{i})=\mathchoice{\displaystyle\mathsf{vrep}}{\textstyle\mathsf{vrep}}{\scriptstyle\mathsf{vrep}}{\scriptscriptstyle\mathsf{vrep}}(\mathchoice{\displaystyle\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}}{\textstyle\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}}{\scriptstyle\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}}{\scriptscriptstyle\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}})=\mathchoice{\displaystyle\mathsf{vrep}}{\textstyle\mathsf{vrep}}{\scriptstyle\mathsf{vrep}}{\scriptscriptstyle\mathsf{vrep}}((\varepsilon_{ij})):=[\varepsilon_{11},\varepsilon_{22},\varepsilon_{33},\sqrt{2}\,\varepsilon_{23},\sqrt{2}\,\varepsilon_{13},\sqrt{2}\,\varepsilon_{12}]^{\mathchoice{\displaystyle\mathsf{T}}{\textstyle\mathsf{T}}{\scriptstyle\mathsf{T}}{\scriptscriptstyle\mathsf{T}}}\in\mathcal{T}.

(125)

This way the elastic energy is
{\mathchoice{{{{\raise 2.58334pt\hbox{{$\textstyle 1$}}}{\raise 1.07639pt\hbox{\kern-1.50696pt\hbox{/}}}{\raise-1.29167pt\hbox{\kern-1.72218pt\hbox{{$\textstyle 2$}}}}}}}{{{{\raise 3.01385pt\hbox{{$\scriptstyle 1$}}}{\raise 0.86108pt\hbox{\kern-1.72218pt\hbox{\footnotesize/}}}{\raise-0.86108pt\hbox{\kern-1.72218pt\hbox{{$\scriptstyle 2$}}}}}}}{{{{\raise 3.22916pt\hbox{{$\scriptscriptstyle 1$}}}{\raise 0.73193pt\hbox{\kern-1.93747pt\hbox{\scriptsize/}}}{\raise-0.6458pt\hbox{\kern-1.72218pt\hbox{{$\scriptscriptstyle 2$}}}}}}}{{{{\raise 3.22916pt\hbox{{$\scriptscriptstyle 1$}}}{\raise 0.73193pt\hbox{\kern-1.93747pt\hbox{\tiny/}}}{\raise-0.6458pt\hbox{\kern-1.72218pt\hbox{{$\scriptscriptstyle 2$}}}}}}}}(\mathchoice{\displaystyle\boldsymbol{\sigma}}{\textstyle\boldsymbol{\sigma}}{\scriptstyle\boldsymbol{\sigma}}{\scriptscriptstyle\boldsymbol{\sigma}}:\mathchoice{\displaystyle\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}}{\textstyle\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}}{\scriptstyle\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}}{\scriptscriptstyle\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}})={\mathchoice{{{{\raise 2.58334pt\hbox{{$\textstyle 1$}}}{\raise 1.07639pt\hbox{\kern-1.50696pt\hbox{/}}}{\raise-1.29167pt\hbox{\kern-1.72218pt\hbox{{$\textstyle 2$}}}}}}}{{{{\raise 3.01385pt\hbox{{$\scriptstyle 1$}}}{\raise 0.86108pt\hbox{\kern-1.72218pt\hbox{\footnotesize/}}}{\raise-0.86108pt\hbox{\kern-1.72218pt\hbox{{$\scriptstyle 2$}}}}}}}{{{{\raise 3.22916pt\hbox{{$\scriptscriptstyle 1$}}}{\raise 0.73193pt\hbox{\kern-1.93747pt\hbox{\scriptsize/}}}{\raise-0.6458pt\hbox{\kern-1.72218pt\hbox{{$\scriptscriptstyle 2$}}}}}}}{{{{\raise 3.22916pt\hbox{{$\scriptscriptstyle 1$}}}{\raise 0.73193pt\hbox{\kern-1.93747pt\hbox{\tiny/}}}{\raise-0.6458pt\hbox{\kern-1.72218pt\hbox{{$\scriptscriptstyle 2$}}}}}}}}(\mathchoice{\displaystyle\mathsfbfit{s}}{\textstyle\mathsfbfit{s}}{\scriptstyle\mathsfbfit{s}}{\scriptscriptstyle\mathsfbfit{s}}\cdot\mathchoice{\displaystyle\mathsfbfit{e}}{\textstyle\mathsfbfit{e}}{\scriptstyle\mathsfbfit{e}}{\scriptscriptstyle\mathsfbfit{e}})={\mathchoice{{{{\raise 2.58334pt\hbox{{$\textstyle 1$}}}{\raise 1.07639pt\hbox{\kern-1.50696pt\hbox{/}}}{\raise-1.29167pt\hbox{\kern-1.72218pt\hbox{{$\textstyle 2$}}}}}}}{{{{\raise 3.01385pt\hbox{{$\scriptstyle 1$}}}{\raise 0.86108pt\hbox{\kern-1.72218pt\hbox{\footnotesize/}}}{\raise-0.86108pt\hbox{\kern-1.72218pt\hbox{{$\scriptstyle 2$}}}}}}}{{{{\raise 3.22916pt\hbox{{$\scriptscriptstyle 1$}}}{\raise 0.73193pt\hbox{\kern-1.93747pt\hbox{\scriptsize/}}}{\raise-0.6458pt\hbox{\kern-1.72218pt\hbox{{$\scriptscriptstyle 2$}}}}}}}{{{{\raise 3.22916pt\hbox{{$\scriptscriptstyle 1$}}}{\raise 0.73193pt\hbox{\kern-1.93747pt\hbox{\tiny/}}}{\raise-0.6458pt\hbox{\kern-1.72218pt\hbox{{$\scriptscriptstyle 2$}}}}}}}}(\mathchoice{\displaystyle\mathsfbfit{s}}{\textstyle\mathsfbfit{s}}{\scriptstyle\mathsfbfit{s}}{\scriptscriptstyle\mathsfbfit{s}}^{\mathchoice{\displaystyle\mathsf{T}}{\textstyle\mathsf{T}}{\scriptstyle\mathsf{T}}{\scriptscriptstyle\mathsf{T}}}\mathchoice{\displaystyle\mathsfbfit{e}}{\textstyle\mathsfbfit{e}}{\scriptstyle\mathsfbfit{e}}{\scriptscriptstyle\mathsfbfit{e}})
. While this would also be true in the Voigt notation,
in the Kelvin notation one has additionally
\mathchoice{\displaystyle\boldsymbol{\sigma}}{\textstyle\boldsymbol{\sigma}}{\scriptstyle\boldsymbol{\sigma}}{\scriptscriptstyle\boldsymbol{\sigma}}:\mathchoice{\displaystyle\boldsymbol{\sigma}}{\textstyle\boldsymbol{\sigma}}{\scriptstyle\boldsymbol{\sigma}}{\scriptscriptstyle\boldsymbol{\sigma}}=\mathchoice{\displaystyle\mathsfbfit{s}}{\textstyle\mathsfbfit{s}}{\scriptstyle\mathsfbfit{s}}{\scriptscriptstyle\mathsfbfit{s}}^{\mathchoice{\displaystyle\mathsf{T}}{\textstyle\mathsf{T}}{\scriptstyle\mathsf{T}}{\scriptscriptstyle\mathsf{T}}}\mathchoice{\displaystyle\mathsfbfit{s}}{\textstyle\mathsfbfit{s}}{\scriptstyle\mathsfbfit{s}}{\scriptscriptstyle\mathsfbfit{s}}
and

\mathchoice{\displaystyle\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}}{\textstyle\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}}{\scriptstyle\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}}{\scriptscriptstyle\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}}:\mathchoice{\displaystyle\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}}{\textstyle\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}}{\scriptstyle\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}}{\scriptscriptstyle\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}}=\mathchoice{\displaystyle\mathsfbfit{e}}{\textstyle\mathsfbfit{e}}{\scriptstyle\mathsfbfit{e}}{\scriptscriptstyle\mathsfbfit{e}}^{\mathchoice{\displaystyle\mathsf{T}}{\textstyle\mathsf{T}}{\scriptstyle\mathsf{T}}{\scriptscriptstyle\mathsf{T}}}\mathchoice{\displaystyle\mathsfbfit{e}}{\textstyle\mathsfbfit{e}}{\scriptstyle\mathsfbfit{e}}{\scriptscriptstyle\mathsfbfit{e}}
, as the same inner product is used for stresses and strains,
facilitating the consideration of eigenvalue problems for the elasticity tensor.

Note that elements of
\mathcal{T}
such as
\mathchoice{\displaystyle\mathsfbfit{s}}{\textstyle\mathsfbfit{s}}{\scriptstyle\mathsfbfit{s}}{\scriptscriptstyle\mathsfbfit{s}}
are denoted by lower case bold sans-serif
letters to distinguish them from vectors in
\mathcal{V}
such as
\mathchoice{\displaystyle\boldsymbol{x}}{\textstyle\boldsymbol{x}}{\scriptstyle\boldsymbol{x}}{\scriptscriptstyle\boldsymbol{x}}
. Linear
maps
\mathscr{L}(\mathcal{T})
on
\mathcal{T}
like the Kelvin matrix
\mathchoice{\displaystyle\mathsfbfit{C}}{\textstyle\mathsfbfit{C}}{\scriptstyle\mathsfbfit{C}}{\scriptscriptstyle\mathsfbfit{C}}\in\mathscr{L}(\mathcal{T})

later in Eq.(
129
) are denoted by upper case bold sans-serif letters
to distinguish them from linear maps on
\mathcal{V}
like
\mathchoice{\displaystyle\boldsymbol{Q}}{\textstyle\boldsymbol{Q}}{\scriptstyle\boldsymbol{Q}}{\scriptscriptstyle\boldsymbol{Q}}\in\mathscr{L}(\mathcal{V})
,
but the matrix notation from
\mathcal{V}\equiv\mathbb{R}^{d}
is also used on
\mathcal{T}\equiv\mathbb{R}^{k}
.

Sometimes it is useful to consider other, slightly different, basis vectors in
\mathcal{T}

than the canonical unit vectors
\mathchoice{\displaystyle\mathsfbfit{e}}{\textstyle\mathsfbfit{e}}{\scriptstyle\mathsfbfit{e}}{\scriptscriptstyle\mathsfbfit{e}}_{1},\dots,\mathchoice{\displaystyle\mathsfbfit{e}}{\textstyle\mathsfbfit{e}}{\scriptstyle\mathsfbfit{e}}{\scriptscriptstyle\mathsfbfit{e}}_{6}\in\mathcal{T}

implied in Eqs.(
124
) and (
125
), which come from the Cartesian
form of the tensors
\mathchoice{\displaystyle\boldsymbol{\sigma}}{\textstyle\boldsymbol{\sigma}}{\scriptstyle\boldsymbol{\sigma}}{\scriptscriptstyle\boldsymbol{\sigma}},\mathchoice{\displaystyle\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}}{\textstyle\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}}{\scriptstyle\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}}{\scriptscriptstyle\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}}\in\mathcal{S}
. Recall that in 3D the trace of the strain
tensor,
\tr\mathchoice{\displaystyle\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}}{\textstyle\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}}{\scriptstyle\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}}{\scriptscriptstyle\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}}
, is the volume
change, and
-{\mathchoice{{{{\raise 2.58334pt\hbox{{$\textstyle 1$}}}{\raise 1.07639pt\hbox{\kern-1.50696pt\hbox{/}}}{\raise-1.29167pt\hbox{\kern-1.72218pt\hbox{{$\textstyle 3$}}}}}}}{{{{\raise 3.01385pt\hbox{{$\scriptstyle 1$}}}{\raise 0.86108pt\hbox{\kern-1.72218pt\hbox{\footnotesize/}}}{\raise-0.86108pt\hbox{\kern-1.72218pt\hbox{{$\scriptstyle 3$}}}}}}}{{{{\raise 3.22916pt\hbox{{$\scriptscriptstyle 1$}}}{\raise 0.73193pt\hbox{\kern-1.93747pt\hbox{\scriptsize/}}}{\raise-0.6458pt\hbox{\kern-1.72218pt\hbox{{$\scriptscriptstyle 3$}}}}}}}{{{{\raise 3.22916pt\hbox{{$\scriptscriptstyle 1$}}}{\raise 0.73193pt\hbox{\kern-1.93747pt\hbox{\tiny/}}}{\raise-0.6458pt\hbox{\kern-1.72218pt\hbox{{$\scriptscriptstyle 3$}}}}}}}}\tr\mathchoice{\displaystyle\boldsymbol{\sigma}}{\textstyle\boldsymbol{\sigma}}{\scriptstyle\boldsymbol{\sigma}}{\scriptscriptstyle\boldsymbol{\sigma}}
is the pressure, and one would like to separate this
from shear effects. To achieve this distinction one may introduce the orthonormal vectors

\displaystyle\mathchoice{\displaystyle\mathsfbfit{n}}{\textstyle\mathsfbfit{n}}{\scriptstyle\mathsfbfit{n}}{\scriptscriptstyle\mathsfbfit{n}}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\sqrt{3}}(\mathchoice{\displaystyle\mathsfbfit{e}}{\textstyle\mathsfbfit{e}}{\scriptstyle\mathsfbfit{e}}{\scriptscriptstyle\mathsfbfit{e}}_{1}+\mathchoice{\displaystyle\mathsfbfit{e}}{\textstyle\mathsfbfit{e}}{\scriptstyle\mathsfbfit{e}}{\scriptscriptstyle\mathsfbfit{e}}_{2}+\mathchoice{\displaystyle\mathsfbfit{e}}{\textstyle\mathsfbfit{e}}{\scriptstyle\mathsfbfit{e}}{\scriptscriptstyle\mathsfbfit{e}}_{3})

\displaystyle{}=

\displaystyle{}\frac{1}{\sqrt{3}}\;[\phantom{-}1,\phantom{-}1,\phantom{-}1,\phantom{-}0,\phantom{-}0,\phantom{-}0]^{\mathchoice{\displaystyle\mathsf{T}}{\textstyle\mathsf{T}}{\scriptstyle\mathsf{T}}{\scriptscriptstyle\mathsf{T}}},

(126)

\displaystyle\mathchoice{\displaystyle\mathsfbfit{y}}{\textstyle\mathsfbfit{y}}{\scriptstyle\mathsfbfit{y}}{\scriptscriptstyle\mathsfbfit{y}}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}(\mathchoice{\displaystyle\mathsfbfit{e}}{\textstyle\mathsfbfit{e}}{\scriptstyle\mathsfbfit{e}}{\scriptscriptstyle\mathsfbfit{e}}_{2}-\mathchoice{\displaystyle\mathsfbfit{e}}{\textstyle\mathsfbfit{e}}{\scriptstyle\mathsfbfit{e}}{\scriptscriptstyle\mathsfbfit{e}}_{1})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}\;[-1,\phantom{-}1,\phantom{-}0,\phantom{-}0,\phantom{-}0,\phantom{-}0]^{\mathchoice{\displaystyle\mathsf{T}}{\textstyle\mathsf{T}}{\scriptstyle\mathsf{T}}{\scriptscriptstyle\mathsf{T}}},\text{ and}

(127)

\displaystyle\mathchoice{\displaystyle\mathsfbfit{z}}{\textstyle\mathsfbfit{z}}{\scriptstyle\mathsfbfit{z}}{\scriptscriptstyle\mathsfbfit{z}}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\sqrt{6}}(\mathchoice{\displaystyle\mathsfbfit{e}}{\textstyle\mathsfbfit{e}}{\scriptstyle\mathsfbfit{e}}{\scriptscriptstyle\mathsfbfit{e}}_{1}+\mathchoice{\displaystyle\mathsfbfit{e}}{\textstyle\mathsfbfit{e}}{\scriptstyle\mathsfbfit{e}}{\scriptscriptstyle\mathsfbfit{e}}_{2}-2\mathchoice{\displaystyle\mathsfbfit{e}}{\textstyle\mathsfbfit{e}}{\scriptstyle\mathsfbfit{e}}{\scriptscriptstyle\mathsfbfit{e}}_{3})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\sqrt{6}}\;[\phantom{-}1,\phantom{-}1,-2,\phantom{-}0,\phantom{-}0,\phantom{-}0]^{\mathchoice{\displaystyle\mathsf{T}}{\textstyle\mathsf{T}}{\scriptstyle\mathsf{T}}{\scriptscriptstyle\mathsf{T}}}.

(128)

Thus a projection on
\mathchoice{\displaystyle\mathsfbfit{n}}{\textstyle\mathsfbfit{n}}{\scriptstyle\mathsfbfit{n}}{\scriptscriptstyle\mathsfbfit{n}}\in\mathcal{T}
yields a quantity proportional to volume
change resp. pressure, and the projections onto the orthonormal vectors

\mathchoice{\displaystyle\mathsfbfit{y}}{\textstyle\mathsfbfit{y}}{\scriptstyle\mathsfbfit{y}}{\scriptscriptstyle\mathsfbfit{y}},\mathchoice{\displaystyle\mathsfbfit{z}}{\textstyle\mathsfbfit{z}}{\scriptstyle\mathsfbfit{z}}{\scriptscriptstyle\mathsfbfit{z}},\mathchoice{\displaystyle\mathsfbfit{e}}{\textstyle\mathsfbfit{e}}{\scriptstyle\mathsfbfit{e}}{\scriptscriptstyle\mathsfbfit{e}}_{4},\mathchoice{\displaystyle\mathsfbfit{e}}{\textstyle\mathsfbfit{e}}{\scriptstyle\mathsfbfit{e}}{\scriptscriptstyle\mathsfbfit{e}}_{5},\mathchoice{\displaystyle\mathsfbfit{e}}{\textstyle\mathsfbfit{e}}{\scriptstyle\mathsfbfit{e}}{\scriptscriptstyle\mathsfbfit{e}}_{6}\in\mathcal{T}
are pure shears.

The linear vector representation map
\mathchoice{\displaystyle\mathsf{vrep}}{\textstyle\mathsf{vrep}}{\scriptstyle\mathsf{vrep}}{\scriptscriptstyle\mathsf{vrep}}:\mathcal{S}\to\mathcal{T}
in
Eqs.(
124
) and (
125
) induces following Eq.(
1
)
a linear representation map

\mathchoice{\displaystyle\mathsf{Vrep}}{\textstyle\mathsf{Vrep}}{\scriptstyle\mathsf{Vrep}}{\scriptscriptstyle\mathsf{Vrep}}:\mathcal{E}\to\mathscr{L}_{s}(\mathcal{T})
, which
maps
\mathchoice{\displaystyle\mathbf{c}}{\textstyle\mathbf{c}}{\scriptstyle\mathbf{c}}{\scriptscriptstyle\mathbf{c}}\in\mathcal{E}
onto a symmetric matrix
\mathchoice{\displaystyle\mathsfbfit{C}}{\textstyle\mathsfbfit{C}}{\scriptstyle\mathsfbfit{C}}{\scriptscriptstyle\mathsfbfit{C}}\in\mathscr{L}_{s}(\mathcal{T})=\mathfrak{sym}(\mathcal{T})
,

\mathchoice{\displaystyle\mathbf{c}}{\textstyle\mathbf{c}}{\scriptstyle\mathbf{c}}{\scriptscriptstyle\mathbf{c}}\mapsto\mathchoice{\displaystyle\mathsfbfit{C}}{\textstyle\mathsfbfit{C}}{\scriptstyle\mathsfbfit{C}}{\scriptscriptstyle\mathsfbfit{C}}=\mathchoice{\displaystyle\mathsf{Vrep}}{\textstyle\mathsf{Vrep}}{\scriptstyle\mathsf{Vrep}}{\scriptscriptstyle\mathsf{Vrep}}(\mathchoice{\displaystyle\mathbf{c}}{\textstyle\mathbf{c}}{\scriptstyle\mathbf{c}}{\scriptscriptstyle\mathbf{c}})
, such that Hooke’s generalised law in Eq.(
123
) becomes

\mathchoice{\displaystyle\mathsfbfit{s}}{\textstyle\mathsfbfit{s}}{\scriptstyle\mathsfbfit{s}}{\scriptscriptstyle\mathsfbfit{s}}=\mathchoice{\displaystyle\mathsfbfit{C}}{\textstyle\mathsfbfit{C}}{\scriptstyle\mathsfbfit{C}}{\scriptscriptstyle\mathsfbfit{C}}\mathchoice{\displaystyle\mathsfbfit{e}}{\textstyle\mathsfbfit{e}}{\scriptstyle\mathsfbfit{e}}{\scriptscriptstyle\mathsfbfit{e}}.

(129)

This map
\mathchoice{\displaystyle\mathsfbfit{C}}{\textstyle\mathsfbfit{C}}{\scriptstyle\mathsfbfit{C}}{\scriptscriptstyle\mathsfbfit{C}}=\mathchoice{\displaystyle\mathsf{Vrep}}{\textstyle\mathsf{Vrep}}{\scriptstyle\mathsf{Vrep}}{\scriptscriptstyle\mathsf{Vrep}}(\mathchoice{\displaystyle\mathbf{c}}{\textstyle\mathbf{c}}{\scriptstyle\mathbf{c}}{\scriptscriptstyle\mathbf{c}})
can be
given in terms of the tensor components of
\mathchoice{\displaystyle\mathbf{c}}{\textstyle\mathbf{c}}{\scriptstyle\mathbf{c}}{\scriptscriptstyle\mathbf{c}}=(\mathrm{c}_{ik\ell m})
,
(i,k,\ell,m,=1,\dots,3)
,
yielding the 3D
Kelvin matrix
Eq.(
12
) with

\dim\mathcal{E}=21
independent components.

Kelvin notation in 2D:

In 2D for
d=2
,
\mathcal{V}=\mathbb{R}^{2}
, and
k=3=\dim\mathcal{S}
, one again chooses
the same basis
[
113
,
118
]
for strains and stresses

\mathchoice{\displaystyle\boldsymbol{\sigma}}{\textstyle\boldsymbol{\sigma}}{\scriptstyle\boldsymbol{\sigma}}{\scriptscriptstyle\boldsymbol{\sigma}},\mathchoice{\displaystyle\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}}{\textstyle\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}}{\scriptstyle\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}}{\scriptscriptstyle\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}}\in\mathcal{S}
, the representation now being — compare
Eqs.(
124
) and (
125
):

\displaystyle\mathchoice{\displaystyle\mathsfbfit{s}}{\textstyle\mathsfbfit{s}}{\scriptstyle\mathsfbfit{s}}{\scriptscriptstyle\mathsfbfit{s}}

\displaystyle:=\mathchoice{\displaystyle\mathsf{vrep}}{\textstyle\mathsf{vrep}}{\scriptstyle\mathsf{vrep}}{\scriptscriptstyle\mathsf{vrep}}(\mathchoice{\displaystyle\boldsymbol{\sigma}}{\textstyle\boldsymbol{\sigma}}{\scriptstyle\boldsymbol{\sigma}}{\scriptscriptstyle\boldsymbol{\sigma}}):=[\sigma_{11},\sigma_{22},\sqrt{2}\,\sigma_{12}]^{\mathchoice{\displaystyle\mathsf{T}}{\textstyle\mathsf{T}}{\scriptstyle\mathsf{T}}{\scriptscriptstyle\mathsf{T}}}\in\mathcal{T}

(130)

\displaystyle\mathchoice{\displaystyle\mathsfbfit{e}}{\textstyle\mathsfbfit{e}}{\scriptstyle\mathsfbfit{e}}{\scriptscriptstyle\mathsfbfit{e}}

\displaystyle:=\mathchoice{\displaystyle\mathsf{vrep}}{\textstyle\mathsf{vrep}}{\scriptstyle\mathsf{vrep}}{\scriptscriptstyle\mathsf{vrep}}(\mathchoice{\displaystyle\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}}{\textstyle\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}}{\scriptstyle\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}}{\scriptscriptstyle\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}}):=[\varepsilon_{11},\varepsilon_{22},\sqrt{2}\,\varepsilon_{12}]^{\mathchoice{\displaystyle\mathsf{T}}{\textstyle\mathsf{T}}{\scriptstyle\mathsf{T}}{\scriptscriptstyle\mathsf{T}}}\in\mathcal{T}.

(131)

As in the 3D case above, sometimes one needs slightly different basis
vectors in
\mathcal{T}
than the canonical unit vectors
\mathchoice{\displaystyle\mathsfbfit{e}}{\textstyle\mathsfbfit{e}}{\scriptstyle\mathsfbfit{e}}{\scriptscriptstyle\mathsfbfit{e}}_{1},\mathchoice{\displaystyle\mathsfbfit{e}}{\textstyle\mathsfbfit{e}}{\scriptstyle\mathsfbfit{e}}{\scriptscriptstyle\mathsfbfit{e}}_{2},\mathchoice{\displaystyle\mathsfbfit{e}}{\textstyle\mathsfbfit{e}}{\scriptstyle\mathsfbfit{e}}{\scriptscriptstyle\mathsfbfit{e}}_{3}\in\mathcal{T}

implied in Eqs.(
130
) and (
131
). In 2D
\tr\mathchoice{\displaystyle\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}}{\textstyle\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}}{\scriptstyle\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}}{\scriptscriptstyle\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}}
is the area
change, and one would like to separate this from shear effects. To this end
introduce the orthonormal vectors

\displaystyle\mathchoice{\displaystyle\mathsfbfit{n}}{\textstyle\mathsfbfit{n}}{\scriptstyle\mathsfbfit{n}}{\scriptscriptstyle\mathsfbfit{n}}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}(\mathchoice{\displaystyle\mathsfbfit{e}}{\textstyle\mathsfbfit{e}}{\scriptstyle\mathsfbfit{e}}{\scriptscriptstyle\mathsfbfit{e}}_{1}+\mathchoice{\displaystyle\mathsfbfit{e}}{\textstyle\mathsfbfit{e}}{\scriptstyle\mathsfbfit{e}}{\scriptscriptstyle\mathsfbfit{e}}_{2})

\displaystyle{}=

\displaystyle{}\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}\;[\phantom{-}1,\phantom{-}1,\phantom{-}0]^{\mathchoice{\displaystyle\mathsf{T}}{\textstyle\mathsf{T}}{\scriptstyle\mathsf{T}}{\scriptscriptstyle\mathsf{T}}},

(132)

\displaystyle\mathchoice{\displaystyle\mathsfbfit{y}}{\textstyle\mathsfbfit{y}}{\scriptstyle\mathsfbfit{y}}{\scriptscriptstyle\mathsfbfit{y}}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}(\mathchoice{\displaystyle\mathsfbfit{e}}{\textstyle\mathsfbfit{e}}{\scriptstyle\mathsfbfit{e}}{\scriptscriptstyle\mathsfbfit{e}}_{2}-\mathchoice{\displaystyle\mathsfbfit{e}}{\textstyle\mathsfbfit{e}}{\scriptstyle\mathsfbfit{e}}{\scriptscriptstyle\mathsfbfit{e}}_{1})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}\;[-1,\phantom{-}1,\phantom{-}0]^{\mathchoice{\displaystyle\mathsf{T}}{\textstyle\mathsf{T}}{\scriptstyle\mathsf{T}}{\scriptscriptstyle\mathsf{T}}}.

(133)

Then a projection on
\mathchoice{\displaystyle\mathsfbfit{n}}{\textstyle\mathsfbfit{n}}{\scriptstyle\mathsfbfit{n}}{\scriptscriptstyle\mathsfbfit{n}}\in\mathcal{T}
yields a quantity proportional to volume
change resp. pressure, and the projections onto the orthonormal vectors

\mathchoice{\displaystyle\mathsfbfit{y}}{\textstyle\mathsfbfit{y}}{\scriptstyle\mathsfbfit{y}}{\scriptscriptstyle\mathsfbfit{y}},\mathchoice{\displaystyle\mathsfbfit{e}}{\textstyle\mathsfbfit{e}}{\scriptstyle\mathsfbfit{e}}{\scriptscriptstyle\mathsfbfit{e}}_{3}\in\mathcal{T}
are pure shears.

Again, the linear vector representation
\mathchoice{\displaystyle\mathsf{vrep}}{\textstyle\mathsf{vrep}}{\scriptstyle\mathsf{vrep}}{\scriptscriptstyle\mathsf{vrep}}:\mathcal{S}\to\mathcal{T}
in
Eqs.(
130
) and (
131
) induces a linear representation

\mathchoice{\displaystyle\mathsf{Vrep}}{\textstyle\mathsf{Vrep}}{\scriptstyle\mathsf{Vrep}}{\scriptscriptstyle\mathsf{Vrep}}:\mathcal{E}\to\mathscr{L}_{s}(\mathcal{T})=\mathfrak{sym}(\mathcal{T})
, mapping
\mathchoice{\displaystyle\mathbf{c}}{\textstyle\mathbf{c}}{\scriptstyle\mathbf{c}}{\scriptscriptstyle\mathbf{c}}\in\mathcal{E}
onto a
matrix
\mathchoice{\displaystyle\mathsfbfit{C}}{\textstyle\mathsfbfit{C}}{\scriptstyle\mathsfbfit{C}}{\scriptscriptstyle\mathsfbfit{C}}\in\mathscr{L}_{s}(\mathcal{T})
, and is given in terms of the
tensor components of
\mathchoice{\displaystyle\mathbf{c}}{\textstyle\mathbf{c}}{\scriptstyle\mathbf{c}}{\scriptscriptstyle\mathbf{c}}=(\mathrm{c}_{ik\ell m})
,
(i,k,\ell,m,=1,\dots,2)
, by
the 2D Kelvin matrix Eq.(
13
) with
\dim\mathcal{E}=6
independent components.
